It's just... it's like the studio lights are on, but there’s no music in me anymore, if that makes sense. I just feel really down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, hollow sadness?\n\nPatient: I'd say... it's been about nine months now. Yeah, nine months of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed a change with that, to be honest. I still enjoy dancing and working on new choreography. It's just... that heavy feeling is always there in the background while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in the pleasure you get from activities you normally enjoy, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, like I said, I still like dancing and putting together routines. It's just that I feel sad while I'm doing it. But the actual enjoyment of the dancing hasn't really gone away or decreased.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really been feeling anxious or worried about anything specific. It's... it's really just that heavy sadness I was talking about. But no, no real anxiety or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty all over the place, honestly. Um... there were some nights where I was just staying up sketching formations until dawn. But lately, it's different. I'll fall asleep completely exhausted, and then I just wake up at four or five in the morning with my stomach feeling really tight.
